## 2026-10-07 AnalysisPanel split (preparation for the new layout)

- **Why**: `components/AnalysisPanel.tsx` had grown to 761 lines mixing store wiring, ranking logic, scroll/observer effects and several JSX sections, which made every layout change risky. This is a behavior-preserving refactor (761 → 436 lines); nothing visible changes.
- **Extracted into `components/analysis-panel/`** (the existing ownership boundary for this panel): pure logic `simpleViewSelection.ts` (verified-card ranking for the simple view), `credibilitySummary.ts` (one verdict from card trust statuses), `nextStep.ts` (which recommended action applies and its copy keys); hooks `useResponsiveColumnCount.ts`, `useCardWindow.ts` (visible-card window, spotlight, auto-scroll, progressive load) and `useSqlPrecheckInspection.ts`; components `ResultsViewToggle.tsx`, `RecommendedNextStep.tsx`, `LargeDatasetNotice.tsx`. `AnalysisPanel` keeps the store subscription, derived flags and layout order.
- **Left in place on purpose**: the big `useAppStore` selector and the early-return "nothing to show" branch, because moving them would change subscription and render behavior.
- **Next**: the dataset summary bar and right-hand rail from the layout plan can now be added as new components in `AnalysisPanel` without touching ranking, scroll or retry logic.
- **Checks**: typecheck, lint, full suite (354 files / 2,899 tests) passed with no change to existing tests; new tests cover next-step priority and copy, view toggle, next-step actions, ranking and credibility, card window and column count hooks. A real-browser run (mocked gateway) rendered the same panel as before. Root deployment regenerated.

## 2026-10-07 Pi agent skills and wider read-only tools

- **Finding that motivated this**: the initial-analysis "agent" has one tool (`run_next_analysis_stage`) and the host forces the exact order, so Pi makes no decisions there; its follow-up agent had only seven tools (`data_query`, `data_describe`, `workspace_list|read|search`, gated `data_mutate`, `analysis_create_plan`). Skills were not used at all (pi-agent-core has a `Skill` type; the app only had code-level "analyst capability skills" mapped to tools).
- **Skills** (`services/agent/skills/`): `skillMarkdown.ts` parses the pi `SKILL.md` format (frontmatter `name`, `description`, optional `disable-model-invocation`; invalid files produce diagnostics, never throws); `skillRegistry.ts` merges bundled skills (`builtin/<name>/SKILL.md`, loaded with `import.meta.glob ?raw`, so static hosting still works) with workspace skills (`skills/<name>/SKILL.md` or `skills/<name>.md`), a workspace skill replacing a built-in of the same name; `skillsPrompt.ts` lists skills with the harness's own `formatSkillsForSystemPrompt` (name and description only, progressive disclosure). `piSkillTool.ts` adds the `read_skill` tool (two reads per request, not counted against the app-tool budget, hides `disable-model-invocation` skills, sees workspace skills added moments ago). `createPiFollowUpSystemPrompt` appends the listing; `createPiAppTools` includes the tool (`includeSkills: false` to omit).
- **Built-in skills**: choose-metric-and-aggregation (do not sum prices or rates), data-quality-check, explore-with-data-query, pivot-and-crosstab, compare-periods-and-find-drivers, clean-data-safely. A test fails if a skill mentions a tool name that does not exist, so skills cannot drift from the tool registry.
- **Tools**: `data.missing`, `data.outliers`, `data.value_counts` are now flagged `readOnly` and `piFollowUpReadOnly` and reach Pi (the capability test was updated on purpose). Follow-up provider turns went from 6 to 8 so a skill read cannot cut an answer short.
- **Not done / honest limits**: (1) The initial analysis still runs the fixed nine-stage pipeline; skills are not injected into the stage prompts yet, and Pi does not plan it. (2) There is no UI to add user skills, so workspace skills are supported by the loader but not reachable by an end user yet (needs a Settings import and persistence). (3) `analysis_pivot_matrix`, `analysis_period_compare`, `analysis_cohort_retention`, `analysis_root_cause_breakdown`, `analysis_correlation`, `data_reshape` are still not exposed to Pi because they create cards or change data through paths the follow-up card tracking and evidence gate do not yet cover; the skills tell Pi to use `data_query` unless those tools are available. (4) Not run against the live gateway; behaviour with a real model is unverified.
- **Checks**: typecheck, lint, full suite (356 files / 2,913 tests) passed.

## 2026-10-07 Agent skills can be added in Settings

- **Why**: the skill loader already read workspace files, but nothing let a person put a skill there, so skills were not user-extensible in practice.
- **What**: `services/agent/skills/userSkillStore.ts` stores skills added in Settings in localStorage (`csv-ai-user-skills-v1`; at most 30 skills of 20,000 characters; each file validated with the same parser, invalid or oversized files are rejected with a reason and never stored; a valid file replaces a stored skill with the same name; a damaged value or unavailable storage falls back to in-memory for the tab). `clearAllLocalBrowserData` already calls `localStorage.clear()`, so "Clear all local data" removes user skills with no extra code. `resolveSkillEntries` gives every skill with its source and applies the precedence built-in, then Settings, then workspace; `resolveAvailableSkills` keeps its old signature for callers (agent prompt and `read_skill` therefore see Settings skills immediately). New `components/modals/AgentSkillsSection.tsx` in Settings: lists skills with Built-in / Yours badges, imports `.md` files, removes your own skills, shows added and rejected files with reasons, and a format hint with an example. Four languages.
- **Behaviour to know**: importing or removing a skill takes effect immediately and is not tied to the "Save Settings" button (Cancel does not undo it). The Settings dialog is reached from the Assistant panel header, and only where the settings surface is enabled (`showSettingsButton`).
- **Not done**: no export, edit-in-place or enable/disable switch per skill; the initial analysis still does not use skills (fixed nine-stage pipeline); skills are still unverified against a live model.
- **Verified**: unit tests for the store, precedence, agent prompt and tool, and the Settings section; a real-browser run imported a skill file, showed it as "Yours", and found it in localStorage after a reload. Typecheck, lint and the full suite passed.

## 2026-10-07 Pi drafts the research plan for the initial analysis

- **Why**: the nine-stage pipeline fixed the order, and the real topic choice came from a one-shot topic call plus string-matching heuristics (or hardcoded "Sum <metric> by <dimension>" topics for read-only large datasets), so Pi decided nothing and skills were unused there.
- **What**: right after `analysis.researchQuestions` passes, `piResearchPlanner.ts` runs a short Pi agent (read-only data tools, `read_skill`, `submit_research_plan`; no `data_mutate`, no card creation; 9 provider turns, 75 s, at most 3 submissions). Its system prompt lists the goal, file and columns and tells it to read `choose-metric-and-aggregation` first. `researchPlan.ts` validates each proposed question against the real columns (known columns, aggregation fits the column type, metric needs an aggregation, no duplicates) and returns specific rejection reasons so Pi can fix and resubmit; at least 2 valid questions are needed. The accepted plan is stored in `initialAnalysisPlan` (cleared on new session and data restore). `dataAnalysisSessionRunner.ts` uses it once, for the same dataset version only, instead of `generateAnalysisTopics` / `buildDeterministicTopics` in the first round, and copies the chosen dimension, metric and comparison onto the hypotheses; later gap rounds are unchanged.
- **Failure behaviour**: a planner failure, timeout or no submission is silent (`PiResearchPlanner` silent-failure record) and the existing planner runs; a user cancellation still stops the run. Tests that pass a stream override do not run the planner unless `options.plannerStream` is given.
- **Honest limits**: (1) not run against the live gateway, so model behaviour is unverified; (2) it adds up to about 9 gateway requests per run against the 20-requests-per-session and 30-per-minute-per-IP demo limits; (3) the plan is not saved in the checkpoint, so a resumed run falls back to the existing planner; (4) stage order is still fixed and skills are not injected into the evidence stage prompts; (5) later gap rounds still use the old topic generator.
- **Checks**: new tests `researchPlan.test.ts`, `piResearchPlanner.test.ts` and a runner test; typecheck, lint and the full suite (358 files / 2,932 tests) pass.

## 2026-10-07 Skills in the evidence stage, plan in the checkpoint, card tools for Pi, Cancel and row counts

- **Skills in the evidence stage**: the evidence query planner (`planGenerator.generateEvidenceQueryPlanWithRetry`) is a single model call without tools, so it cannot call `read_skill`. `services/agent/skills/evidencePromptSkills.ts` builds an `analysis_skills` prompt section (medium priority, sticky) with the full text of `choose-metric-and-aggregation` and `explore-with-data-query` plus every skill the person added in Settings (skills that set `disable-model-invocation` are left out; total capped at 6,000 characters). The choice of the two built-ins is a fixed list in that file, not a model decision. Workspace-file skills are not included because the planner has no store access.
- **Plan in the checkpoint**: the checkpoint now carries `researchPlan` (optional, schema version unchanged, so older checkpoints still load). A resumed run puts it back into `initialAnalysisPlan`; a fresh run clears any earlier plan first.
- **Card-creating tools for Pi**: manifests of `analysis.pivot_matrix`, `period_compare`, `cohort_retention`, `root_cause_breakdown` and `correlation` carry `piFollowUpCardCreation`; `createPiAppTools` offers them only together with `analysis.create_plan` (`allowCardCreation`), never in the planner, and treats `artifacts.cardId` (these tools) or `createdCardId` (create_plan) as the created card so the existing card tracking, card target and trust labelling apply. The SQL evidence value gate skips cards without a query trace, so these cards keep the automatic quality verdict only. The two card-request prompts in `piContext.ts` now name the tools and when to use each.
- **Cancel and row counts**: a "Stop analysis" button (Analysis status section) is shown while `initialAnalysisStatus` is `running`; it calls `requestInitialAnalysisCancellation` → `cancelPiInitialAnalysis(sessionId)`, which aborts the run and ends as `cancelled` / `paused` with the last stable dataset kept. The older unused `cancelPiInitialAnalysis` was replaced. Each stage now reports `rowCount` (the working dataset's real rows: `backing.rowCount` or `data.length`) on the task status and the in-progress card shows "N rows in the current dataset"; it changes after cleaning because it is read at the start of each stage. The stage-8 research "Stop research" button remains for runs that are not the initial analysis.
- **Checks**: typecheck, lint, full suite (360 files / 2,943 tests) pass; the checkpoint restore test fails when the restore line is removed.
- **Not done / honest limits**: (1) none of this was run against the live gateway; (2) there is no per-stage row-count *change* ("before → after") and no per-stage timer; (3) Stop cancels at the next safe point, so a running query or model call may finish first; (4) pivot and other card tools use the three-tool-call-per-turn budget shared with data tools; (5) the evidence-stage skill list is fixed to two built-ins; (6) gap rounds still use the old topic generator.

## 2026-10-07 Pi chooses stages, designs evidence queries, and a regex/hardcode audit

- **Pi chooses stages (initial analysis)**: `run_next_analysis_stage` now takes optional `stage` and `reason`. With no arguments it runs the next required stage, as before. Pi may skip only `dataset.suggestCleaningPlan` and `dataset.applyTransform`, and only by naming a later stage whose preceding stages are all optional; every other stage is required, so a run can never reach evidence without a prepared, validated dataset. After each stage the tool result lists `nextRequired`, `optionalStagesYouMaySkip` and real `facts` (rows, columns, residual noise candidates, data-quality note count). The system prompt tells Pi to skip cleaning only when no noise candidates and no notes exist, and when unsure not to skip. An invalid request returns an error naming the allowed choices; after two invalid requests in a row the host runs the next required stage itself. A skip is an agent event (`pi_skipped_optional_stages`), not a user-facing warning. The checkpoint index still works because only forward skips are allowed.
- **Pi designs the evidence query**: `pi/piEvidencePlanner.ts` runs a short Pi agent per research question (read-only data tools capped at 2 calls, `read_skill`, `submit_evidence_plan` whose parameters are the existing evidence plan schema; 4 provider turns, 50 s, 3 submissions). The plan is validated by the same `normalizeAndValidateSqlEvidenceQueryPlan` as the one-shot planner, but without the SUM-to-AVG text rewrite, so the aggregation Pi chose in the research plan survives. It is used only when a Pi research plan drove the run, for the first attempt (pre-generation, two questions at a time because the demo gateway allows 2 concurrent requests); on any failure the stepped planner runs as before. Retries after a failed execution still use the old planners.
- **Cost to know**: each research question can use up to 4 more gateway requests, on top of the research planner (up to 9) and stage turns (9+). With the demo limits (20 requests per session, 30 per minute per IP) this is close to the ceiling; it is not run against the live gateway yet, so real timing and rate-limit behaviour are unverified. A user key avoids the shared limits.
- **Audit of remaining regex/keyword decisions** (read-only review; nothing below was changed). Highest impact first; "keep" items are safety checks:
  1. `planning/evidenceQuerySemantics.ts` (RATIO/AVERAGE/CUMULATIVE patterns): silently rewrites SUM to AVG from topic text. Replace with: AI proposes the aggregation, code verifies hard facts (profile type percentage, user annotation), override becomes a visible note. (Pi-designed plans already skip this rewrite.)
  2. `data/columnRegistry.ts` `NON_ADDITIVE_NAME_PATTERNS` and 3. `runtime/rowRoleSignals.ts` `NON_ADDITIVE_FACT_COLUMN_PATTERN`: the same English keyword list decides additivity in two places; headers in other languages are not recognised. Replace with an AI column annotation (flow, stock, ratio) enforced by SQL validation; keep the regex only as a prior.
  4. `agent/reportShapeUtils.ts` footer/subtotal/total patterns: English-only row-noise detection that feeds row exclusion. Replace with AI row-role labels verified by reconciliation (already partly present).
  5. `runtime/hierarchyDetector.ts` `SUBTOTAL_KEYWORDS` confirms a hierarchy by keyword alone ("Gross Sales" mislabelled). Verify by numeric reconciliation instead.
  6. `reportShapeDetector.ts`, `reportShapeVerification.ts`, `reportStructureNormalization.ts`, `reportShapeSummary.ts`: series-header patterns include tokens tied to sample files (`corp`, `ec`, `re`, `capex`). Delete those, verify proposed series columns structurally.
  7. `planning/planGenerator.ts` `buildDeterministicTopics` / `scoreDeterministicMetric`: name-keyword scoring picks the primary metric when AI topics fail, and always averages time trends. Use the semantic `candidateMetrics` annotation or count-only topics.
  8. `planGenerator.ts` `rankAnalysisTopics` / `scoreTopicBusinessValue`: keyword scores and a `by uom` hack order the cards. Let the topic AI return priority and rationale.
  9. `runtime/hypothesisBuilder.ts` `inferMentionedValue`, `topicMentionsColumn`, `comparisonIntent` regex: text-to-column mapping by string overlap (Pi plans now carry structured fields, which removes this for planned runs).
  10. `planning/semanticTextMatching.ts` `COUNT_INTENT_PATTERN`, `TIME_INTENT_PATTERN`, `IDENTIFIER_LIKE_COLUMN_PATTERN` ("order", "no" misfire) and `evidencePlanAdapter.inferPreferredResultShape`: shape the stepped plan from text.
  11. `runtime/analystCapabilityResolver.ts`, `pivotDecision.ts`: chat tool exposure by English keyword; Pi should choose from tool and skill descriptions with code gating only by preconditions and safety.
  12. `derivedMetricIntent.ts`, `execution/createPlanPreflight.ts`: derived-metric intent by regex; replace with the agent declaring the formula and coverage requirement (`derivedMetricDeclaration.ts` exists).
  13-22 (medium): `data/csvParser.ts` header and unit-code gates, `columnRoleClassifier.ts` name override, `reportShapeCodeHierarchy.ts` WBS-style code pattern (copied in three files), `datasetSemantics.ts` report-type guess, `evidenceValueGate.ts` hard reject on `zero_total_value` and English date formats, `analysisColumnRoles.ts` time-name pattern (copied in four files), `goalProposer.ts` score re-ranking, `numericReconciliation.ts` descriptor/unit name lists, `data/dataProfiler.ts` currency symbols and decimal format, `investigationDirectives.ts` code/label names.
  23-25 (low, keep): pipeline-internal helper-column names, relative-date phrases, intent-signal hints.
- **Keep as code (already well designed)**: evidence gate duplicate and type-mismatch rejects, numeric reconciliation, structure-proposal verifier, SQL validation, exact-match internal helper-column sets. Recommended order for follow-up work: 1-3, then 7-9, then 4-6.
- **Honest status**: this is a pipeline with agentic parts, not an end-to-end agent. Pi now chooses between cleaning and skipping, plans the questions, and designs each query with tools and skills; the stage order, the cleaning, structure detection, the evidence gates and the summary stage are still code, and many items above are still regex. None of this was run against the live gateway.
- **Checks**: typecheck, lint and the full suite (361 files / 2,951 tests) pass; new tests for the stage choice (skip, refusal of a required stage, auto-advance after repeated invalid requests), the evidence planner (valid plan keeps the chosen aggregation, retry after an invalid plan, no submission, cancellation) and the runner hook (Pi plan used, not run without a research plan).

## 2026-10-07 Additivity judged by Pi and verified by code; skills can be exported, edited and switched off

- **Audit items 1-3 (additivity)**: `ColumnProfile.additivity` (`kind` additive or non_additive, `nature` flow, stock, ratio, unit_value or other, short rationale) now holds Pi's judgement of whether a numeric column can be summed. The research planner asks for it in the same `submit_research_plan` call (`columns`, optional), so it costs no extra gateway request. `validateColumnAdditivity` keeps only what the app can stand behind: the column must exist and be numeric, kind and nature must be valid, a ratio or unit value cannot be additive, and a percentage column is always non-additive whatever the model says. Accepted judgements are merged into `columnProfiles`. Evidence order is now `isNonAdditiveMetric`: percentage type, then Pi's judgement, then (only when neither exists) the old column-name keywords as a prior. This feeds the column registry's `aggregationHint` (which the SQL governance check enforces) and `isRatioLikeMetric` (the SUM-to-AVG rewrite), so an additive "margin" in dollars is no longer treated as a ratio and a balance Pi marks non-additive is no longer summed. The duplicate keyword list in `rowRoleSignals.ts` was replaced by the shared `isNonAdditiveMetricName` (it also fixes substring hits such as "Corporate" matching "rate").
- **Limits of item 3**: `rowRoleSignals` runs during structure detection, before Pi has seen the data, so it can only use the keyword prior. The judgement is stored on profiles, so a later re-profiling of the dataset drops it until the next run; the column-name prior then applies again. Users cannot override a judgement in the UI yet. Not run against the live gateway. Audit items 4-25 are untouched.
- **Skills in Settings**: every skill row now has an on/off switch (stored in localStorage `csv-ai-disabled-skills-v1`; a switched-off skill is not listed to Pi, cannot be read with `read_skill`, and is left out of the evidence-stage guidance), Edit (a text editor validated like an import; saving a built-in skill stores your version under the same name, Restore default removes it; renaming removes the old copy), and Export (downloads `<name>.md` that can be imported again). Four languages.
- **Checks**: typecheck, lint and the full suite (362 files / 2,963 tests) pass; new tests for additivity validation, precedence and registry hint (`columnAdditivity.test.ts`), the planner attaching judgements, and the skill switch, edit, restore and export.

## 2026-10-07 Audit items 4-6 re-checked: one real fix, two corrections

- **Item 6 (series columns) fixed**: `reportShapeDetector.ts` recognised a report's detail series columns partly by header words tied to sample files (`corp_`, `corp`, `ec`, `re`, `project`, `office`, `capex`). Those words are removed. A column now also counts as a series column when its values below the header are numeric (at least 3 values in the first 300 rows, at least 70% numeric-looking, parentheses read as negatives), which is checked from the data instead of guessed from the name. The generic period words (months, FY, Q1-4, ISO year-month) stay as a prior. A text column called "Project" or "Office" is no longer taken as a series. The test fails on the old code. This path applies only to files with parser-detected header layers and at least four series columns.
- **Item 4 (row noise) was overstated in the audit**: the row inspection is already tiered. Structural signals (sum verification against detail rows, sparsity, position) classify first, and the label regexes act as a fallback or an extra signal. New tests show that a total row labelled "合计" or "Jumlah" is still found because its numbers reconcile, and ordinary records named "Net Wireless" or "Total Facility Engineering" stay detail rows. One caution remains: a row with an explicit "sub-total" label (`SUBTOTAL_ROW_PATTERN`) is classified as a subtotal at 0.96 even when it does not reconcile, by design (position-shifted exports). I did not change that without real files to judge it against.
- **Item 5 (hierarchy) was overstated**: `detectHierarchyGroups` finds parents only where children's totals reconcile with the parent within 2% (at least two children, 95% coverage), then an AI call confirms or rejects. The subtotal keyword list is used only when the AI is unavailable or fails, and only on top of the reconciliation. It is not changed. A possible follow-up is replacing the free-text "Group N: CONFIRMED" parsing of the AI answer with structured output.
- **Honest scope**: this removes one overfit, and re-labels two audit items as mostly fine. Items 7-25 of the audit are untouched. The audit's review was a read-through, not a test of each file; claims about items 4 and 5 are now backed by the new tests only for the cases above.
- **Checks**: typecheck, lint and the full suite (364 files / 2,967 tests) pass; new tests `reportShapeSeriesColumns.test.ts` and `rowInspectionNonEnglish.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-07 Audit items 7-9: fallback topics, topic order, hypothesis intent

- **Item 8 (topic order)**: `rankAnalysisTopics` no longer scores topics by words (money words +4, time words +2, a `by uom|curr` penalty). The topic prompt and schema now ask the model to list topics from most to least useful for a decision-maker, and the order is kept. Code still removes duplicates and topics on blocked dimensions, and still moves topics that touch quality-governed columns (`avoidGrainColumns`, `avoidMetricColumns`, from the semantic understanding) to the end instead of dropping them. The test that encoded the old word ranking was replaced by one that checks the AI order is kept. Risk to watch: a mechanical topic the model lists early (for example a breakdown by unit) is no longer pushed down by code; the prompt now says to prefer meaningful measures, but this is unverified with a live model.
- **Item 7 (fallback topics)**: `buildDeterministicTopics` (used when AI topic generation fails, and for read-only large datasets without a Pi plan) picks the lead metric from facts and the AI's earlier semantic understanding only: a currency-typed column, then the preferred-metric and candidate-metric ranks. The name-keyword scores (`amount|price|revenue…` up, `age|area|lease…` down) are removed. The fallback time topic no longer says "Average"; it reads "<metric> trend by <time column>" so the aggregation is chosen later by the planner and its skills instead of being fixed to AVG. A regex that checked whether a trend topic already existed became a structural check. Limit: with no semantic hints and no currency column, the first numeric column leads, so a physical measure such as an area can lead where the old name penalty would have avoided it.
- **Item 9 (hypothesis intent)**: the keyword guess for `comparisonIntent` is removed (it is now null unless a Pi research plan question supplies it; nothing else in the app read it). `inferMentionedValue` and `topicMentionsColumn` are unchanged: they map topic text to existing columns by exact or normalised containment, which is a verification against real columns, but they still cannot handle synonyms or other languages. Pi research plans already carry structured dimension, metric and aggregation, which avoids this path; the plan-less path remains text matching.
- **Not done**: items 10-25. The plan-less topic path is still string based. Not run against the live gateway.
- **Checks**: typecheck, lint and the full suite (364 files / 2,968 tests) pass; tests updated on purpose: the deterministic trend wording, and the ranking test (now "keeps the AI order"); a new test for lead-metric choice without name words.

## 2026-10-08 Fix: Pi runs failed at stage 1 with Google ("Custom fetch is not supported")

- **Symptom** (user report): with the Google provider the initial analysis stopped at `stage 1/9 "Structure"` with `Custom fetch is not supported by the Google Generative AI adapter`. The AI SDK calls (structure and boundary detection) worked; only the Pi path failed.
- **Cause**: `createPiProviderStream` and the compaction call always passed a custom `fetch` (the User-Agent workaround) to Pi. Pi's Google adapter throws when `options.fetch` is anything but the platform fetch. Pre-existing, unrelated to the `gemini-3.8-flash` addition.
- **Fix**: `resolvePiProviderFetch(settings)` in `piProvider.ts` returns the gateway fetch for Default, the User-Agent-stripping fetch for OpenAI and `undefined` for Google; used by both the stream and the context compactor. Verified against the real adapter in a temporary test (custom fetch gives the exact error; no fetch proceeds to the Google API and returns its own API-key error), and a permanent test covers the helper. Pi Browser Lab (`piBrowserAgent.ts`) is OpenAI-only and unchanged.
- **Not verified**: no real Google key was used, so a full Google analysis on Pi is unconfirmed. Branch `claude/pi-google-no-custom-fetch`, local only; not deployed.

## 2026-10-08 Google on Pi: history must not end with a model turn; gemini-3.8-flash confirmed

- **Confirmed**: the user's own run returned `modelVersion: gemini-3.8-flash` (goal proposal), so the id added earlier exists and the key works.
- **Symptom**: Pi stage 8 on Google failed with HTTP 400 `Requests ending with a model turn are not supported.`
- **Evidence for the cause**: a temporary test against the real Pi Google adapter showed that a history ending with a text-only assistant turn is sent with `contents` roles `user,model` (last = model, which Gemini rejects), while a history ending with a tool result is sent as `user,model,user` (accepted). Pi continues after a text-only model turn in several loops, so such a request can occur.
- **Fix**: `ensureGoogleHistoryEndsWithUserTurn` in `piProvider.ts` appends a user turn (`Continue.`) when the transformed history ends with an assistant message; `createPiProviderContextTransform` applies it for the Google provider only, so every Pi agent (initial analysis, research and evidence planners, follow-up) is covered. Test added in `piProductionRuntime.test.ts`.
- **Not verified**: which exact Pi loop produced the failing request in the user's run was not captured (the turn-trace branch `claude/pi-turn-trace` would show it), and no full Google analysis was run here. Branch `claude/pi-google-trailing-model-turn`, local only.

## 2026-10-08 DuckDB worker reset no longer loses the loaded dataset

- **Cause chain** (from the user's Google + full-HDB log and the code): a Pi `data.describe` over 982k rows hit the 8 s tool timeout; `DuckDbWorkerClient` terminates the worker on a timeout (the only way to stop a runaway query), which discarded the in-memory table; every later Pi tool call then failed with `Table with name session_clean_dataset does not exist`, Pi spent its turns on failing tools and the turn guard ended the run ("Pi did not complete the governed stage sequence").
- **Fix** (`services/workers/duckDbWorkerClient.ts`): the client remembers the last successful `loadCleanDataset` / `loadFileDataset` payload (references only, no copy; a `File` is a handle). A reset (timeout, abort, crash, unhealthy worker) marks it for restore; the next `executeCompiledQuery` / `executeRawQuery` re-creates the worker, reloads the dataset (concurrent callers share one restore) and logs `Restored table ...`. A new load, a failed load or `disposeDuckDbSession` clears the memory, so a stale dataset is never restored. Timeout termination is unchanged.
- **Checks**: new `duckDbWorkerRestoreAfterReset.test.ts` (fails without the fix, passes with it) plus the existing worker and query-engine tests (108 in 13 files), typecheck, lint baseline, `publish:root` (77 files), `test:publish` 4/4.
- **Not verified**: not reproduced in a real browser with the real DuckDB worker; a restore of the 983k-row file takes a few seconds (the initial load took about 5 s), which the next query now absorbs. The slow `data.describe` itself and the `SourceRowIndex` binder error are NOT fixed. Branch `claude/duckdb-restore-after-reset`, local only.
- **Process note**: untracked generated files survive branch switches; a leftover sample CSV leaked into one publish and was removed before this commit (restore the generated files from git, then re-run `publish:root`).

## 2026-10-08 Where the gateway requests go (small sample, 42 requests, 155 s)

- **Tool**: `services/ai/demoGatewayRequestLog.ts` keeps a 300-entry in-memory log of each Default-gateway request's shape (system-prompt start, tool names, structured-output schema name, input item count; never message text), recorded in `fetchDefaultGateway` once per logical request. Read it in the browser with `__demoGatewayLog()`.
- **Measured** (local build, gateway calls proxied to the Pages origin, "Load Small HDB Sample", ended "Results available: 3 verified, 2 need review"): 9 x stage orchestrator (`run_next_analysis_stage`, the only job is to say "run the next stage"; 21%), 8 x BI analyst Markdown (per-card summaries), 5 x evidence query designer, 4 x research planner, 3 x goal proposer, 2 + 2 x prepared-data readiness / semantic classification, 2 x hierarchy confirmation, 5 x report summaries, 1 x boundary detector, 1 x report context extractor. The earlier un-logged run of the same sample used 34 and 44 requests, so the count varies by run.
- **Implication**: only 2 of the 9 stages are optional (cleaning), so 7 to 8 orchestrator requests carry no decision. Letting the host run required stages without an LLM turn, and consulting Pi only at the optional-skip decision, is the largest safe saving (about a fifth of requests). Summaries (about a third) are the next block.

## 2026-10-09 Session isolation and current-turn query grounding

- **Result:** implemented the approved PI-001/PI-002 plan locally. A shared session epoch/run lease fences initial analysis, follow-up preflight and execution, clarification/approval resumes, asynchronous store actions, streams and terminal cleanup. History/New Session cancels the outgoing task, preserves completed content, serializes persistence and installs only the latest transition. A cancelled old finalizer cannot end a newer run.
- **Resources/persistence:** DuckDB generations and a bind/query queue isolate restore/dispose and cache state; semantic in-flight ownership includes session epoch and dataset version; vector callbacks/storage, sandbox and OPFS cleanup use captured scope/namespaces. Autosave, checkpoints, report/intake snapshots and the current-session alias use the same persistence queue. Leases are not saved. Load/archive errors keep the outgoing content with the task cancelled.
- **Grounding:** host-owned query receipts capture session/run/turn/tool-call and dataset binding before execution. ## 2026-10-09 Checkpoint recovery and source reattachment

- **Authorization/result:** user selected option D (checkpoint recovery). This corresponds to review PI-004; PI-005 concerns gateway session acquisition and remains outside this fix. Preserved the existing session isolation/grounding, intake repair and unrelated user modifications.
- **Checkpoint contract:** v2 payload under the compatible v1 storage key separates immutable source identity/boundary from latest prepared and canonical material versions. Ordered committed stage receipts, operation/artifact lineage fingerprints and the research plan define a verified stable resume boundary. Snapshot persistence and checkpoint metadata use the same captured state; stage idempotency uses the actual input version. Recovery obtains a fresh lease and does not rerun completed mutations. Legacy checkpoints require matching prepared rows; uncertain/malformed boundaries fail closed.
- **Metadata-only recovery:** boot and History inspect checkpoints with no loaded rows and expose `awaiting_source`. The existing upload action routes checkpoint sources through fingerprint verification, deterministic committed declarative replay, canonical reconstruction and DuckDB rebind, then resumes the next stage. Wrong source, changed lineage, unavailable binding or unsupported replay preserves saved content and shows a blocked reason; no automatic fresh analysis starts. Delayed reads and finalizers are fenced by the existing session/run coordinator.
- **Required persistence fixes:** cleaning records its actual prepared input version even when the bundle describes a different canonical view; replay validates input and output versions. Root-browser reload also reproduced the serializer treating repeated references as cycles and dropping shared operation payloads. Serialization now tracks only ancestors, retaining the same operation in plan and lineage while still removing genuine cycles. Existing records whose payload was already lost cannot be reconstructed safely and remain blocked.
- **Verification:** official SHA-256-verified Node 22.13.1. Deferred real-Pi/tool-loop stale-version regression failed before repair and passed afterwards, continuing at validatePreparedData without applying the mutation twice. Final targeted checkpoint/Pi/serializer/replay group 51/51 passed, including canonical/prepared separation, serialized hydration, wrong-file retry, missing receipts, malformed checkpoints, rebind failure and session-switch races. Final full suite 400/400 files, 3,707/3,707 tests; typecheck and lint passed. Publisher tests 4/4 passed.
- **Checked-in app:** published root PWA 1.0.0-b051263b7ea5; all 78 checksums and the local Pages staging allowlist verified. Actual Chrome root app, synthetic seeded stable checkpoint and local Responses SSE mock verified metadata-only reload/awaiting_source, same-name/same-size wrong-source rejection without a provider call, corrected reattachment, raw string preservation and numeric prepared replay, real DuckDB binding, next-stage execution under a fresh lease, and Stop cleanup with saved content retained. ## 2026-10-09 Default gateway cancellation and deadlines

- **Authorization/result:** user selected option A (PI-005 gateway session acquisition cancellation/deadlines). Preserved prior session isolation/grounding, intake and checkpoint repairs, and unrelated dirty work. No commit/push/Pages dispatch.
- **Gateway lifecycle:** shared `/demo/session` acquisition, including JSON/error-body reads, now has a 15-second abortable deadline. Each waiter observes its own signal; the shared controller stops only when the last waiter leaves or the deadline expires. Identity-checked cache publication/finalization prevents abandoned late work from replacing or clearing a newer refresh. Already-aborted callers do not contact the gateway.
- **Request/slot contract:** Default POST acquisition/header budget is 45 seconds across queue waits, handshake/refresh, response headers and retry delays. Signal races return even for non-cooperating fetch implementations and cancel discarded late response bodies. Queued cancellation removes the waiter; every failure releases its slot. Returned streams retain caller cancellation and the existing 10-minute body safety limit, cancel their reader on termination and release exactly once. Final Default serialized payload still omits max_output_tokens/top_p and uses the session token/model. No SDK/provider/context policy changes.
- **Verification:** official SHA-256-verified Node 22.13.1. Five deferred/fake-timer regressions failed before repair. Final targeted gateway/production-runtime group 70/70 passed, covering first acquisition, 401 refresh, shared cancellation, late refresh fencing, stalled session/error-body reads, queue deadline, retry, unresponsive responses fetch, late response disposal and stream slot cleanup. Actual Pi adapter stream.result() terminates on Stop and handshake/SDK deadlines; its wire regression passed. Strict test context was corrected to SDK normalizeContext; Pi tests reran 4/4 and final typecheck/lint passed. Full suite 402 files/3,726 tests passed; publisher tests 4/4.
- **Checked-in app:** published PWA 1.0.0-427335abd325; all 78 checksums and local Pages staging allowlist verified. Actual Chrome root app, synthetic two-row seed and local-only mocks verified 15-second stalled-handshake timeout with transport abort/busy false, real Stop-button cancellation of a real Pi run before handshake release, lease removal and retained rows. Actual AI SDK shared waiters verified independent abort, continued valid caller, stale-handshake fencing and a usable subsequent request with correct serialized fields. ## 2026-10-09 Coding-agent capability expectation

- **Explicit user direction:** the CSV Data Analysis Chatbox should provide coding-agent-style data analysis: inspect the app's dataset/schema and available system resources, choose and execute queries, load reusable skills, use extensible tools such as MCP integrations, and author/run/verify corrective logic for unfamiliar malformed CSVs (including shifted cells and subtotal rows). The desired reasoning and repair workflow should adapt to evidence instead of requiring a separately hard-coded business repair for each file shape.
- **Verified current boundary:** main follow-up chat constructs a real Pi `Agent` from `@earendil-works/pi-agent-core` with `pi-ai` provider integration. It is not the full `pi-coding-agent` CLI environment. `piAppTools.ts` selects read-only data diagnostics/query and workspace reader manifests, conditionally exposes card creation, and includes deterministic mutation proposals routed through approval. Ordinary app-tool budget is three; batch analysis is eight. Skills are real built-in/Settings/workspace Markdown guidance loaded through `read_skill`, but cannot grant new capabilities.
- **Existing partial coding support:** initial analysis calls a staged cleaning pipeline whose verified fallback generates Python/JavaScript transformations and executes browser workers. Follow-up chat has no general sandbox-code execution or workspace write/edit exposure. No MCP client/discovery bridge or operating-system shell/filesystem execution was found in the active application services. DuckDB queries operate on the app's browser-bound datasets; this is not an external database administration connection.
- **Target interpretation:** provide an agent-driven inspect → hypothesize → write/query → execute → validate → revise → commit workflow over a session-scoped task workspace. Keep ownership, immutable source data, evidence grounding and appropriate mutation approval as host contracts. Skills supply methods, while tools/executors supply actual access. A full OS process/package environment or external database/MCP access requires a separately designed execution/connection layer beyond the current static browser app; no backend destination, credential access or deployment is authorized by this clarification.
- **Evidence/scope:** checked local runtime/tool/skill/cleaning code and installed SDK documentation; upstream Pi documentation separately identifies agent-core as the runtime and coding-agent as the CLI (https://github.com/earendil-works/pi). Prior generated-root runtime tests remain the execution evidence; this turn did not run a new browser/provider test or change application source/build. README now states the current boundary. This is a product expectation clarification, not a claim that the full capability has been implemented.
